### WEEK 6 — MY SENSES AND SIMPLE SAFETY

*Depth: school-generated — the school's own subtopic list for N2 S2 W6.*

**You will learn to**

• name the five senses and the organ of each
• use the senses to know a thing safely
• keep away from the dangers of the home
• cross the road the right way and know the stranger rule

**Things to know**

• The five **senses** and their **organs**: **sight — eyes** · **hearing — ears** · **smell — nose** · **taste — tongue** · **touch/feel — skin (hands)**.
• What each tells me: the eye tells **colour and shape**; the ear tells **sound and direction**; the nose tells **smell** (a good soup, a gas leak). The tongue tells **taste** — sweet, sour, bitter, salty; the skin tells **hot, cold, rough, smooth, sharp, pain**.
• The senses warn us: a **smell of smoke** tells us to run, a **hot cup** tells the hand to let go, a **bad smell** on milk tells us not to drink it.
• Never test a thing with the wrong sense first: **do not taste or smell an unknown powder; look, then ask** — the nose can be burned by acid fumes.
• Care of the senses: no sharp thing in the ear, no staring at the sun, no loud sound near the ear, eyes kept an arm's length from a screen, hands washed before eating.
• **Safety at home** — the four dangers of a child: **fire** (matches, kerosene stove, burning coal), **hot water** (the pot on the floor. The bath mixed with cold first), **sharp objects** (knife, cutlass, broken glass, razor, nail), **medicines** (a pill that looks like a sweet is not a sweet).
• Home rules: matches are **left where they are**, not picked up; nothing is touched on the cooking table. A broken glass is **reported and swept by a grown-up**, not held in the hand; medicine is taken only when it is given, never from the shelf.
• **Road safety**: hold a grown-up's **hand, not the finger**; stop at the kerb; **look left, right, left again**; **listen** for the engine. Cross at the **bridge (footbridge) or the zebra corner**; never run across and never chase a ball into the road.
• **Stranger danger**: never **follow** a stranger, never **take gifts, sweets or a ride**, never go into a stranger's room or car, shout **"help!"** loudly if somebody holds you. And **tell somebody the same hour** about anything a grown-up asked you to keep secret.
• The **safe and unsafe picture game**: a green card for safe, a red card for unsafe — the hand on the pot is red. The grown-up's hand held is green, the sweet from a stranger is red, the crossing at the bridge is green.
• Safety songs and the class poster: our own poster has four pictures and four rules, and it is put on the door so the whole school sees it.

**Words for my notebook**

Eyes see, ears hear, nose smells, tongue tastes, skin feels. Red card = danger: fire, hot water, sharp things, medicine, strangers.

**Let us talk**

1. Which sense tells you that milk has turned?
2. Why must you not taste an unknown powder?
3. What are the four dangers of the home?
4. Show me how you look before you cross the road.

**Worksheet**

1. The organ of hearing is the A) skin B) ear C) nose D) tongue
2. Before we cross the road we should A) close our eyes B) run C) look left, right and left again D) shout
3. A stranger gives you a sweet and asks you to come with him. You should A) take the sweet and look back B) thank him quietly C) go with him D) refuse, shout and tell
4. A broken glass on the floor should be A) left for the next person B) picked up with the hand C) kicked aside D) reported so that a grown-up sweeps it
5. Fill in: The sense of taste is in the ____.
6. Mention the five senses with the organ of each.
7. Write two sentences on one safety rule you will keep this week.

**My own work**

Look round your house and write two safe things and two unsafe things you can see; put a tick beside the one you will move or report.

### WEEK 8 — NIGERIAN ANIMALS, BIRDS AND THEIR HOMES

*Depth: school-generated — the school's own subtopic list for N2 S2 W8.*

**You will learn to**

• name farm animals in Nigeria and what they give us
• put each animal in the home we build for it
• name our national bird and the king of animals
• match an animal to its sound and to its home

**Things to know**

• Farm animals and their uses: **goat** (meat and milk, and it eats the weeds round the house), **cow** (milk, meat, and its dung makes the farm ground rich), **chicken** (eggs and meat. And its feathers), **sheep** (wool and meat), **pig** (meat), **rabbit** (meat and fur), and beside them the **duck** (eggs) and the **guinea fowl** (eggs and meat — the common protein of the north).
• What each gives: **eggs** from bird and fowl · **milk** from the cow and the goat · **meat** from nearly all of them · **hide and leather** from the cow and goat · **wool** from the sheep · **manure** from all of them for the farm.
• Animals and their homes: the **dog** lives in a **kennel** · the **chicken** in a **coop (poultry house)** · the **cow** in a **shed/byre** · the **goat** in a **pen** · the **pig** in a **sty** · the **rabbit** in a **hutch** · the **horse** in a **stable** · the **sheep** in a **fold** · and the bush animals make their own: the bird a **nest**, the rat a **burrow**, the python a **hole**, the monkey the **trees**.
• Our **national bird is the eagle** — it is on our coat of arms with the two horses. The eagle **sees far**, so it stands for strength and vision; its house is the top of a tall tree or a rock.
• The **lion** is called the **king of animals** because he walks with his head up and the rest of the bush makes way for him; his sound shakes the bush and his home is the open savannah.
• Other animals of Nigeria to know: the **elephant** (biggest land animal, in the north-east), the **leopard**, the **buffalo**, the **hippo** in the river. The **crocodile**, the **gorilla and chimpanzee** in the Cross River forests, the **python**, the **tortoise** of our stories.
• Animals that live with us in stories: the **tortoise (Ìjàpá)** — clever but greedy · the **rabbit** — quick but easily frightened · the **lion** — strong but to be flattered · and in the tales, the little animal beats the big one with the head, not the leg.
• **Picture matching**: animal to home (dog—kennel), animal to sound (hen—gèé, cock—kòkòrò, goat—mèé, cow—moooo, dog—gbáà! ), animal to baby (cow—calf, goat—kid, sheep—lamb, hen—chick).
• Kindness rule: an animal that lives with us is **fed, watered, kept clean and not tied too short**; a bird's egg taken from the nest for play is a bird that will not come back to our street.

**Words for my notebook**

Dog—kennel · chicken—coop · cow—shed · goat—pen · bird—nest. The eagle is our national bird; the lion is the king of animals.

**Let us talk**

1. Which two animals give us milk and eggs?
2. What is the home of the dog called?
3. Which bird is on our coat of arms?
4. What does the tortoise teach us in the stories?

**Worksheet**

1. The home of a dog is called a A) kennel B) hutch C) coop D) sty
2. The bird that lays our eggs and lives in a coop is the A) eagle B) hen C) owl D) parrot
3. Our national bird, which is on the coat of arms, is the A) eagle B) hen C) pigeon D) vulture
4. Which animal is called the king of animals? A) the elephant B) the leopard C) the buffalo D) the lion
5. Fill in: The home of a rabbit is called a ____.
6. Name four farm animals and one thing each gives us.
7. Write two sentences on an animal you know, its home and its sound.

**My own work**

Draw four animals and draw their homes beside them, then write the sound of two of the animals as you hear it.

### WEEK 9 — SONGS, RHYMES AND STORIES OF NIGERIA

*Depth: school-generated — the school's own subtopic list for N2 S2 W9.*

**You will learn to**

• sing Nigerian nursery rhymes and a lullaby
• listen to a folktale and say what it teaches
• hear the first lines of our anthem
• retell a story in my own words in the circle

**Things to know**

• A **nursery rhyme** is a small song with a game in it: *"Two two two, eye eye eye"* type action songs, *"Àlọ́, àlọ́! Àlọ́ ni!"* and the counting songs we clap.
• A **lullaby** is the song a mother sings to make a child sleep; it is soft, slow, and it names the child: *"Sleep, my little one; the sun has gone; the bird is in the tree; sleep, sleep, sleep."*
• Nigerian **folk songs** are for work and for play: the **pounding song** (with the pestle), the **water-seller's song**, the **farmer's song**, the **harvest song**, and the dance songs of each people.
• **Folktale 1 — the tortoise and the wisdom of the world**: Ìjàpá put all the world's wisdom in a gourd and went up a tree to hide it, the gourd tied on his front. His small son called out, "Tie it on your back!" — and Ìjàpá knew that a child had more sense than he. He dropped the gourd. It broke, and the wisdom flew out and was scattered for everybody. **Lesson: no person is wise enough alone; wisdom is shared, and a child may teach an elder.**.
• **Why the lizard nods**: the anole (lizard) keeps his head up and down because he once carried a message he forgot on the way — so he nods to ask whether it was right. **Lesson: do what you are sent to do, and finish it.**
• Tales that teach, with their one word: **honesty** (the woodcutter's axe that was not his) · **sharing** (the pot that fed the village) · **obedience** (the girl who did not listen and followed the strange bird) · **hard work** (the two farms — one weeded daily, one left to grass).
• Songs about Nigeria: the **anthem "Arise, O compatriots"** — today only the **first listening**: stand, be quiet, hear the melody once, and say one word you heard (*"compatriots", "toil", "serve", "peace", "justice"*). Our pledge is not yet ours to write, but it is ours to stand for.
• A second Nigeria song for us: *"Nigeria, my country, the land of my birth; / green is the farm and the flag is my worth."* — one verse, with the hand on the chest.
• **Storytelling circle**: one child is the teller, the rest answer *"Àlọ́ ni!"*. The teller shows a picture card, tells the tale in **four lines** — who, what happened, what went wrong, what we learn — then the class **retells** it in their own words, one line each.
• A story that is retold becomes yours; a story that is only heard stays in the class.

**Words for my notebook**

Rhyme, lullaby, folk song, àlọ́. Wisdom is for everybody. "Arise, O compatriots" is our national anthem.

**Let us talk**

1. What is a lullaby for?
2. Who carried the world's wisdom in a gourd?
3. What lesson did the tale teach?
4. Name one Nigerian song and when it is sung.

**Worksheet**

1. The song a mother sings to make a child sleep is a A) march B) work song C) lullaby D) harvest song
2. In the tale, the animal that carried all the world's wisdom was the A) tortoise B) lion C) dog D) rabbit
3. The tortoise's little son taught him that wisdom should be A) hidden in a tree B) sold C) shared and not hidden D) forgotten
4. Before we retell a tale we say the call A) "Ọ́ dàbọ̀!" B) "Káàlẹ̀!" C) "Ẹ̀ ṣé!" D) "Àlọ́!"
5. Fill in: "Arise, O ____" is the national anthem of ____.
6. Name four things that tales teach us, and one tale for each.
7. Write four lines of a story you retold in the circle: who, what happened, what went wrong, what we learn.

**My own work**

Choose one tale from this week, tell it at home to whoever is there, and write the four lines of it in your book before Friday.

Draw the animals in your favourite Nigerian tale and colour each one.

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — BASIC SCIENCE (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. Food is anything we eat or drink that gives the body ___
   A) work for our hands only
   B) a bad smell and colour
   C) energy and help to grow
   D) only a full belly and nothing else
2. The meal we take in the morning before coming to school is ___
   A) lunch
   B) breakfast
   C) dinner
   D) supper
3. The meal we take in the middle of the day is called ___
   A) supper
   B) breakfast
   C) tea
   D) lunch
4. Yam, rice, beans and plantain are foods that come from ___
   A) plants
   B) stones
   C) water only
   D) animals
5. Eggs, milk, meat and fish are foods that come from ___
   A) plants
   B) trees
   C) animals
   D) rivers only
6. Living things are things that ___
   A) never move or feed
   B) have life in them
   C) are made in a factory
   D) cannot grow at all
7. Which of these is a non-living thing?
   A) a mango tree
   B) a catfish
   C) a snail
   D) a piece of chalk
8. Which is NOT one of the signs of life?
   A) feeding and breathing
   B) having young ones
   C) shining brightly in the dark
   D) moving and growing
9. A fish breathes inside water with its ___
   A) ears
   B) gills
   C) nose
   D) scales
10. A snail goes back into its shell when you touch it. This shows that living things ___
   A) answer what happens around them
   B) never move at all by themselves
   C) do not need any air at all
   D) make their own houses of clay
11. The young one of a dog is called a ___
   A) kitten
   B) calf
   C) puppy
   D) chick
12. A frog begins its life in water as a ___
   A) chrysalis
   B) tadpole
   C) caterpillar
   D) puppy
13. Between the caterpillar and the butterfly comes the stage called the ___
   A) chrysalis
   B) tadpole
   C) young fowl
   D) seedling
14. A baby animal that takes milk from its mother is called a ___
   A) fish
   B) bird
   C) reptile
   D) mammal
15. The first growth of a seed, when the root comes out, is called ___
   A) pollination
   B) germination
   C) boiling
   D) harvesting
16. The part of a young plant that grows down into the soil is the ___
   A) fruit
   B) leaf
   C) root
   D) flower
17. Which of these is NOT needed for a plant to grow?
   A) air
   B) space to spread
   C) sunlight
   D) a basket of sweets
18. Water that is clean and safe for drinking is ___
   A) clear and without bad smell
   B) full of floating dirt
   C) warm like soup
   D) thick with red colour
19. A canoe and a ferry are means of transport on ___
   A) sand
   B) road
   C) water
   D) rail
20. The thing that keeps a passenger safe inside a moving car is the ___
   A) big shoe
   B) seat belt
   C) school bag
   D) sun cap
21. Our bones give the body ___
   A) shape, support and protection
   B) colour and smell
   C) nothing at all at all
   D) only a bad hardness
22. The bone of the head that protects the brain is called the ___
   A) spine
   B) kneecap
   C) rib
   D) skull
23. The bones of the chest that keep the heart and the lungs safe are the ___
   A) skulls
   B) ribs
   C) fingers
   D) ankles
24. The bone of the back that lets us bend and keeps us upright is the ___
   A) spine
   B) skull
   C) wrist
   D) elbow
25. To keep our bones strong we should take ___
   A) bread with no butter
   B) sweets and chalk
   C) only cold water
   D) milk, eggs, fish and beans
26. Technology means the ___
   A) songs that we sing in the class
   B) stories that the elders tell at night
   C) tools and machines people make to work
   D) things that only grow on the farm
27. A mobile phone is useful in an emergency because it can ___
   A) carry a load to the market
   B) call for help for a sick person
   C) cook food for the family
   D) keep our bones straight
28. Making water that is dirty safe for drinking is called ___
   A) pollution
   B) germination
   C) transportation
   D) purification
29. Boiling water is done mainly to ___
   A) kill the germs inside it
   B) make the water heavier
   C) cool the kettle down
   D) add more dirt to it
30. After water has been boiled it should be ___
   A) drunk while it is still hot
   B) kept open in the sun
   C) left to cool in a clean covered cup
   D) poured back in the bucket

## Section B — Sub-objective (10 marks)
Answer briefly.

31. Give three reasons why we eat food.
32. Name the three main meals of the day.
33. Mention four foods that come from plants and two that come from animals.

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — CULTURAL AND CREATIVE ARTS (CCA) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A hand fan is used to ___
   A) keep us cool by moving air
   B) cut paper into pieces
   C) carry books to class
   D) dry our wet clothes in the rain
2. The two kinds of fan we can make in class are the paddle fan and the ___
   A) ceiling fan
   B) round fan
   C) folding fan
   D) window fan
3. The stick we hold a fan by is called its ___
   A) spine
   B) handle
   C) wing
   D) cover
4. Thick paper used for a paddle fan is ___
   A) cardboard
   B) a wet napkin
   C) a thin polythene bag
   D) a soft handkerchief
5. A design that repeats itself is called a ___
   A) story
   B) picture
   C) pattern
   D) letter
6. Colour splash art is made by ___
   A) cutting the paper with scissors
   B) sewing two pieces of cloth
   C) drawing only straight ruler lines
   D) dropping and flicking paint on paper
7. Before splashing paint we should first ___
   A) sweep the whole of the floor
   B) put on an apron and cover the table
   C) wash our hands with the soap
   D) eat our lunch inside the room
8. Blue and yellow mixed together will give the colour ___
   A) brown
   B) black
   C) green
   D) orange
9. Art that shows a feeling instead of a thing is called ___
   A) a poster
   B) a copy book
   C) a portrait
   D) abstract art
10. A hedgehog is covered on its back with ___
   A) spikes
   B) feathers
   C) long hair
   D) scales
11. The round part of a hedgehog's body ends in front in a ___
   A) flat shell
   B) long tail
   C) pointed nose
   D) big ear
12. To make spikes on a paper hedgehog we can use ___
   A) a comb and water
   B) a fork dipped in paint
   C) chalk and a duster
   D) a spoon and a plate
13. Using paint, paper and twigs together in one picture is called ___
   A) mixed media art
   B) only drawing
   C) flat printing
   D) a sand collage
14. How a thing would feel is called its ___
   A) number
   B) colour
   C) name
   D) texture
15. A picture made by sticking torn and cut paper on a surface is a ___
   A) mural
   B) collage
   C) model
   D) mosaic
16. Tearing paper gives an edge that is ___
   A) hard and straight
   B) wet and heavy
   C) soft and feathered
   D) hot and sharp
17. On the friendship tree, one leaf carries ___
   A) the name of a friend
   B) the price of the tree
   C) a drawing of a bird
   D) only our class number
18. A paper bead is made by rolling paper and then ___
   A) boiling the paper in water
   B) putting it in a cold tin
   C) sewing it on a piece of cloth
   D) gluing the tip and letting it dry
19. Jewellery worn round the wrist is called a ___
   A) necklace
   B) anklet
   C) bracelet
   D) ring
20. When we thread beads in an order that repeats, we are making a ___
   A) pattern
   B) poem
   C) picture
   D) promise
21. A foam collage differs from a paper collage because it ___
   A) needs no glue at all
   B) must be painted with water
   C) lies perfectly flat always
   D) rises up from the paper in layers
22. Putting one piece of foam above another to make a picture look thick is ___
   A) shading
   B) layering
   C) tearing
   D) counting
23. In a sand collage, sand is sprinkled on the picture ___
   A) before any drawing is made
   B) instead of using paper
   C) after glue is put on that part
   D) only on the table edge
24. Sand, clay and shells used in art are called ___
   A) factory materials
   B) painted materials
   C) old materials
   D) natural materials
25. Extra sand on a sand picture is ___
   A) shaken off back into the tin
   B) left to cover the drawing
   C) blown on the floor
   D) watered with the plants
26. A cone hat is rolled from paper and held with ___
   A) only our hands
   B) glue, tape or staples
   C) a wet piece of cloth
   D) a heavy stone
27. An elastic is put round the hat to ___
   A) make it heavier
   B) hide the colour
   C) keep it on the head
   D) shorten the cone
28. Walking round the class wearing the hats we made is called a ___
   A) hat parade
   B) hat market
   C) hat counting
   D) hat lesson
29. Glued work should be tested only after it has ___
   A) been wetted again
   B) been left in the sun
   C) been folded
   D) dried well
30. A craft foam sheet is a material that feels ___
   A) thin like a handkerchief
   B) soft and thick
   C) hard like a stone
   D) wet and cold

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — CHRISTIAN RELIGIOUS STUDIES (CRS) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. God is called our Father in ___
   A) our school only
   B) heaven
   C) the river
   D) the market
2. A father at home is different from God because God ___
   A) is a spirit and never dies
   B) eats our food
   C) sleeps in the afternoon
   D) lives in another house
3. God's love for us is said to be perfect and ___
   A) small
   B) far away
   C) everlasting
   D) changing
4. God gives us food, rain and the people who keep us. This shows that God ___
   A) forgets small children
   B) cares and provides
   C) needs our help
   D) only watches from far
5. Prayer is best described as ___
   A) talking to God
   B) singing to a friend
   C) sleeping quietly
   D) reading a book aloud
6. We can pray ___
   A) once in the whole week
   B) only when we are in trouble
   C) only inside a church building
   D) at any time and in any place
7. When we do wrong and say sorry to God, He ___
   A) punishes every child
   B) forgives us
   C) forgets us
   D) leaves us alone
8. The prayer Jesus taught His disciples is called ___
   A) the morning song
   B) the harvest prayer
   C) the Lord's Prayer
   D) the school pledge
9. "God, thank You for today. Forgive my wrong. Help me tomorrow." This short talk with God is a ___
   A) prayer
   B) picture
   C) lesson
   D) song
10. Children of God are told to call God ___
   A) Master only
   B) Stranger
   C) Neighbour
   D) Father
11. God knows my name, my face and my ___
   A) desk number
   B) bag colour
   C) thoughts
   D) shoes only
12. Because God is with us, we are never ___
   A) alone
   B) late
   C) hungry
   D) small
13. Trusting God is like leaning on ___
   A) a tall ladder
   B) a strong wall
   C) a wet cloth
   D) a weak rope
14. The Bible says, "Children, ____ your parents."
   A) forget
   B) follow nowhere
   C) fear only
   D) obey
15. Times we are told to pray every day are ___
   A) Saturday morning only
   B) when it is raining hard
   C) morning, table and night
   D) once in the whole month
16. Before we eat, we ___ God for the food.
   A) owe
   B) thank
   C) blame
   D) forget
17. At home a child shows respect to his father and mother by ___
   A) answering them politely
   B) shouting at them
   C) hiding from them
   D) ignoring them
18. Which of these is NOT the behaviour of a child of God?
   A) sharing things
   B) saying sorry
   C) being friendly
   D) telling lies always
19. When somebody hurts us and says sorry, a child of God should ___
   A) take his bag
   B) fight him twice
   C) forgive him
   D) never speak to him
20. On the Lord's day, Christians go to ___
   A) the market to sell
   B) church to worship God
   C) the river to swim
   D) the farm to plough
21. Offerings in church should be given with a ___
   A) cheerful heart
   B) sleepy head
   C) angry look
   D) crying face
22. Taking care of the animals and trees God made is part of ___
   A) keeping a shop
   B) making a market
   C) winning a prize
   D) caring for creation
23. Which of these spoils the environment God made?
   A) watering the plants
   B) pouring refuse in the gutter
   C) keeping the drain open
   D) sweeping the yard
24. Jesus is called the ____ of God.
   A) brother
   B) cousin
   C) Son
   D) servant only
25. Jesus was born in the town of ___.
   A) Bethlehem
   B) Kano
   C) Jericho
   D) Ibadan
26. A miracle is a work that ___
   A) needs big machines
   B) any child can do
   C) a doctor always does
   D) only God can do
27. The Bible books that first tell us about Jesus are Matthew, Mark, Luke and ___.
   A) Joshua
   B) John
   C) Acts
   D) Romans
28. Jesus taught the people mostly by ___
   A) stories called parables
   B) singing songs alone
   C) drawing on the wall
   D) long written letters
29. Jesus said, "Suffer the little ____ to come unto me."
   A) kings
   B) birds
   C) children
   D) dogs
30. In the story of the lost son, the father ___ when his son came back.
   A) took his clothes
   B) shut the door on him
   C) sent him away
   D) ran out to embrace him

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — BASIC DIGITAL LITERACY (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. The old counting frame with beads on wires is called the ___
   A) monitor
   B) abacus
   C) printer
   D) keyboard
2. Charles Babbage is remembered because he made the ___
   A) steam engine
   B) printing press
   C) first mobile phone
   D) plan of a counting machine
3. The woman who wrote the first steps for a machine was ___
   A) Ada Lovelace
   B) Margaret Ekpo
   C) Queen Amina
   D) Funmilayo Ransome-Kuti
4. As computers changed, they became smaller and ___
   A) slower
   B) dearer
   C) faster
   D) heavier
5. A machine of the fifth generation can learn and answer questions; this is called ___
   A) a paper tape reader
   B) artificial intelligence
   C) a slow old typewriter
   D) a hand-driven generator
6. Hardware means the parts of a computer that we can ___
   A) print on paper
   B) hear but not see
   C) only dream of
   D) touch and see
7. Which of these is software?
   A) a drawing app
   B) the mouse
   C) the keyboard
   D) the power cable
8. The brain of the computer is the ___
   A) monitor
   B) fan
   C) CPU
   D) cover
9. The part that keeps our work in the computer until we call it again is the ___
   A) power switch
   B) screen glass
   C) mouse pad
   D) hard drive
10. Work kept in RAM is lost when ___
   A) we close the file
   B) the power goes off
   C) the fan stops
   D) the desk is swept
11. The big board inside the machine that joins all the parts is the ___
   A) speaker
   B) lamp
   C) motherboard
   D) keyboard
12. The keys used for typing on a computer are the ___
   A) keyboard
   B) scanner
   C) projector
   D) printer
13. The order of letters on a keyboard is called the ___
   A) Friday layout
   B) QWERTY layout
   C) ABC layout
   D) number layout
14. The key that moves us to the next line is ___
   A) Esc
   B) Caps Lock
   C) Shift
   D) Enter
15. To make all the letters big we press ___
   A) Caps Lock
   B) the space bar
   C) Backspace
   D) the wheel
16. Clicking once on the mouse is for ___
   A) shutting the machine down
   B) cooling the CPU
   C) choosing something
   D) printing a page
17. To open a folder we usually ___
   A) shake the mouse
   B) double-click it
   C) pull the cable
   D) press Caps Lock
18. Moving an icon by holding the button, moving the mouse and letting go is ___
   A) scanning
   B) saving
   C) printing
   D) drag and drop
19. A device that takes our voice into the computer is the ___
   A) microphone
   B) fan
   C) monitor
   D) printer
20. While speaking into a microphone we should ___
   A) drop it on the table for fun
   B) shout straight into it very close
   C) hold it by the body, not the cable
   D) put it under running water
21. The right sitting position for computer work keeps our ___
   A) hands wet with water
   B) back straight and eyes level
   C) shoes clean and laced up
   D) bag closed on the floor
22. A device that puts a paper drawing into the computer is the ___
   A) speaker
   B) projector
   C) mouse
   D) scanner
23. A small camera on top of the screen that shows our face on a call is the ___
   A) printer
   B) router
   C) webcam
   D) keyboard
24. A device joined to the computer with no wire at all uses ___
   A) a long rope
   B) Bluetooth
   C) a nail
   D) glue
25. A flash drive carries ___
   A) food for lunch
   B) water for the plant
   C) chalk for the board
   D) our work in the pocket
26. A monitor shows us ___
   A) the work of the computer on a screen
   B) the papers that need cutting
   C) the dust inside the box
   D) the sound of the machine only
27. A blurry picture on a screen means the picture has ___
   A) too many small dots
   B) too few small dots

28. The printer that squirts tiny drops of liquid is the ___
   A) laser printer
   B) typewriter

   D) inkjet printer
29. Too much paper in a printer tray will make the printer ___
   A) wake up
   B) sing
   C) jam
   D) cool down
30. The sound from a headphone is heard by ___
   A) only the person wearing it
   B) everybody in the room
   C) nobody at all
   D) the whole class

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — NIGERIAN HISTORY (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A hero is a person we ___
   A) admire for the way he helps others
   B) fear because he is very strong
   C) pay money to come and see
   D) avoid whenever he is kind
2. A woman who is a hero is called a ___
   A) queen mother
   B) market woman
   C) heroine
   D) princess only
3. Doing the right thing when your heart is beating fast with fear is ___
   A) pride
   B) rest
   C) play
   D) bravery
4. Selfless service means helping others ___
   A) only when you are paid
   B) without asking for a reward
   C) once and never again
   D) for a big prize
5. Which of these is NOT a quality of a hero?
   A) being kind to the sorrowful
   B) keeping one's own good word
   C) taking things from the weak
   D) always telling people the truth
6. People who keep our class clean and sweep the market before dawn are ___
   A) everyday heroes
   B) only workmen
   C) not important
   D) strangers to us
7. Teachers are heroes in our community because they ___
   A) sell books to us
   B) give us knowledge every day
   C) close the school gate
   D) drive us home
8. The people who run into smoke to put out a fire are ___
   A) farmers
   B) teachers
   C) traders
   D) firefighters
9. A police officer is a hero because he ___
   A) cooks for the street
   B) sells fuel
   C) keeps us safe from thieves
   D) sweeps the gutter
10. An everyday hero at home is ___
   A) a father or mother who works for us
   B) a man we only see on TV
   C) the driver of a big bus
   D) a stranger at the market gate
11. A national hero is a person whose work was for ___
   A) one market
   B) his own family
   C) one shop only
   D) the whole country
12. Herbert Macaulay was born in Lagos in the year ___
   A) 1999
   B) 1864
   C) 1960
   D) 2006
13. Herbert Macaulay was trained as a ___
   A) surveyor and an engineer
   B) soldier with a gun
   C) town doctor and healer
   D) motor bus driver
14. Macaulay formed the first political ___ in Nigeria.
   A) farm
   B) school
   C) party
   D) army
15. Macaulay is remembered because he used the newspaper, the court and ___ to fight for the people
   A) fighting with guns
   B) hiding in the bush
   C) leaving the country
   D) speaking and protesting
16. Nnamdi Azikiwe was called Zik of ___
   A) Africa
   B) the school
   C) Lagos
   D) Europe
17. Azikiwe used a newspaper called The West African ___ to teach the people.
   A) Paper
   B) Pilot
   C) Pilot Bird
   D) Press
18. Azikiwe became the first ____ of Nigeria.
   A) captain
   B) farmer
   C) president
   D) chief
19. Azikiwe was born in the year ___
   A) 1904
   B) 2015
   C) 1960
   D) 1999
20. Queen Amina ruled the land of ___
   A) Benin only
   B) Warri
   C) Kano
   D) Zazzau, now Zaria
21. Queen Amina is remembered for the ___
   A) ships she built
   B) school she opened
   C) city walls round the towns
   D) bridges over the sea
22. The walls built in Queen Amina's time are called ___
   A) gele
   B) ganuwa
   C) agbada
   D) iro
23. Funmilayo Ransome-Kuti came from the town of ___
   A) Sokoto
   B) Jos
   C) Onitsha
   D) Abeokuta
24. She led the market women against ___
   A) free schooling
   B) the price of cloth
   C) an unfair tax
   D) the new road
25. Margaret Ekpo organised the market women of ___
   A) Badagry
   B) Aba
   C) Calabar only
   D) Zaria
26. Volunteering means ___
   A) offering to help before you are asked
   B) telling another child to work
   C) refusing a duty
   D) waiting until you are paid
27. Which of these is a small act of service at school?
   A) hiding his book
   B) pushing him in the queue
   C) carrying a friend's heavy bag
   D) taking his chalk
28. Cleaning the gutter of the school helps the community because ___
   A) nobody will ever see us do it
   B) it looks very busy to the visitors
   C) we can skip our own class today
   D) the water will flow and sickness falls
29. Keeping our environment clean and helping the old are part of ___
   A) keeping a secret
   B) being a good citizen
   C) winning a prize
   D) making a market
30. A desk in the classroom belongs to everybody, so it is called ___
   A) public property
   B) a teacher's bag
   C) a stranger's good
   D) my own property

37. Give four acts of service we can do at home without being told.
38. Name four things we can do for elderly people.
39. Mention four ways of keeping our community clean.

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — PHYSICAL AND HEALTH EDUCATION (PHE) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. Feelings of the heart such as joy, fear and anger are called ___
   A) muscles
   B) emotions
   C) games
   D) rules
2. The feeling of being sure that you can do a thing is called ___
   A) sleep
   B) anger
   C) confidence
   D) pride only
3. When we lose a game and do not fight or cry wildly, we show good ___
   A) sportsmanship
   B) noise
   C) dress
   D) speed
4. Nervousness before a race is best beaten by ___
   A) skipping breakfast
   B) shouting at friends
   C) running away from it
   D) practising the race more
5. A hard feeling some children get when they lose is ___
   A) patience
   B) excitement
   C) disappointment
   D) contentment
6. Working together with your team toward one goal is called ___
   A) sleeping
   B) teamwork
   C) hiding
   D) counting
7. Welcoming a new child who does not know the rules of the game is ___
   A) hospitality
   B) fear
   C) pride
   D) anger
8. Accepting another child's mistake without laughing at him is ___
   A) cheating
   B) speed
   C) noise
   D) tolerance
9. Playing by the rules and taking what the one who judges says is ___
   A) shouting
   B) fairness
   C) luck
   D) pride
10. Sometimes leading and sometimes following in a game teaches us ___
   A) singing
   B) reading
   C) leadership
   D) drawing
11. Marching is best described as ___
   A) organised walking in rhythm and formation
   B) running about in any way you like
   C) hopping on only one leg around
   D) lying down quietly on the mat
12. The foot that starts when we march forward is the ___
   A) back foot
   B) left foot
   C) right foot
   D) both at once
13. The command that makes everybody stand straight and keep quiet is ___
   A) Play
   B) Halt
   C) Attention
   D) Rest
14. Keeping an arm's length between us in the line is done to ___
   A) look taller
   B) reach the gate first
   C) hear the drum
   D) avoid kicking another child
15. Marching is good for the body because it develops ___
   A) a loud and a shouting voice
   B) posture, discipline and coordination
   C) having a very big and round belly
   D) strong nails on both of the hands
16. Swinging and twisting the body before running are done to ___
   A) waste some time
   B) cool the class down
   C) warm the muscles first
   D) finish the lesson early
17. A jump should be landed with the knees ___
   A) lifted high up to the chest
   B) crossed behind the other leg
   C) straight and locked at all times
   D) bent, so that the landing is soft
18. Bending sideways with one arm over the head stretches the ___
   A) waist and the sides of the trunk
   B) hair and the top of the head
   C) toes and the nails only
   D) ears and the small fingers
19. Loud, jerky movements in exercise are dangerous because they ___
   A) make us run much more faster
   B) keep the whole class quite cool
   C) can pull a muscle or hurt a joint
   D) help us to breathe better
20. A race run on a marked path is called a ___
   A) field day
   B) marching drill
   C) board game
   D) track event
21. After crossing the finishing line we should ___
   A) keep going a little and then walk
   B) sit right down there at once
   C) lie flat down on the track
   D) turn and run back quickly
22. The first thing to do with a machine that gives smoke is to ___
   A) blow the smoke away with hands
   B) leave it and tell the one in charge
   C) pull the cable out very hard
   D) pour some cold water on it
23. Running with a partner side by side to the line is called ___
   A) relay singing
   B) hopping
   C) marching
   D) running in pairs
24. Climbing is safest when ___
   A) you climb fast with your eyes shut
   B) two children climb one above the other
   C) three points stay on the ladder while one moves
   D) you carry a bag in your hands
25. Looking up before climbing to choose which rung to use is a way of ___
   A) solving the problem
   B) warming the feet
   C) resting the arms
   D) cooling the head
26. Posture means the way we ___
   A) write our names in the book
   B) hold our body when we sit or stand
   C) choose the shoes for the day
   D) eat our food in the morning
27. While sitting to write, the feet should be ___
   A) up on the chair
   B) hanging in the air
   C) crossed behind
   D) flat on the floor
28. The position we should avoid while sleeping, because it twists the neck, is lying ___
   A) on the tummy
   B) flat on the back
   C) on the side
   D) half sitting up
29. A school bag is best carried on ___
   A) one shoulder only
   B) both shoulders
   C) the head always
   D) the fingers
30. Indoor games are mostly good for training the ___
   A) legs only
   B) lungs only
   C) mind and patience
   D) voice

32. Name four hard feelings a game can bring.
33. State four things a game teaches us about living with people.
34. Give four marching commands we use in class.
35. Mention four kinds of body exercise learnt this term.
36. Name four safety rules for climbing.
37. Mention two kinds of start in a short race and the rule about the lane.
38. State four rules for good sitting and standing posture.
39. Name five indoor games we can play in a room.

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — PREVOCATIONAL STUDIES (PVS) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A farmer is a person who ___
   A) drives a big bus to the town
   B) sells cloth inside the market
   C) grows crops or keeps animals for food
   D) mends our shoes at the roadside
2. The farmer who keeps birds for eggs and meat is a ___
   A) poultry farmer
   B) fish farmer
   C) tree farmer
   D) miner
3. Work that begins a new farm, before any seed is planted, is ___
   A) selling the produce
   B) drying the grain
   C) harvesting the crop
   D) clearing the land
4. After planting, the farmer removes the ___
   A) stones from the market
   B) weeds
   C) birds from the sky
   D) roof from the shed
5. The last work in growing a crop, before the money comes, is ___
   A) watering the weeds
   B) plucking the roof
   C) harvesting and selling
   D) burning the fence
6. A fish farmer feeds his fish at set times and keeps the ___
   A) water clean and moving
   B) birds away from the roof
   C) tools in the sun
   D) fire in the shed
7. Why does a farmer begin his work very early in the morning?
   A) because the sun is brighter at noon
   B) because the body is cooler and weeds are soft
   C) because the tools are heavier in rain
   D) because the market is shut down later
8. A farm visit is when the class goes to the farm to ___
   A) play football with the bigger boys
   B) have a long sleep on the grass
   C) buy plenty of food for the house
   D) see with the eyes what the class was told
9. Before we start a farm visit we should ___
   A) carry our lunch in an open bag
   B) run to the gate first
   C) shut our shoes and fill the water flask
   D) leave our books on the ground
10. On the farm we must not ___
   A) pick the fruit or chase the birds away
   B) wash our hands when we come back
   C) look at the plants in the rows
   D) ask the farmer any good question
11. An animal that sits apart from the others in the house is probably ___
   A) sleeping well
   B) happy
   C) the newest
   D) sick
12. A good question to ask the farmer on a visit is ___
   A) what is your father's name?
   B) when did you plant this crop?
   C) how old is your gate?
   D) where do you sleep?
13. A farm tool is a thing we use to ___
   A) make farm work easier
   B) sew the school uniform
   C) cook the family food
   D) wash our plates
14. The tool with a broad blade used for tilling and making ridges is the ___
   A) basket
   B) watering can
   C) hoe
   D) rake
15. A cutlass is used mainly for ___
   A) carrying the harvest to the market
   B) watering the young seedlings daily
   C) sowing the small seeds in rows
   D) clearing the grass and small sticks
16. A tool with teeth for gathering leaves and leveling a seedbed is the ___
   A) rake
   B) spade
   C) cutlass
   D) basket
17. A watering can has a spout with small holes called a ___
   A) leaf
   B) handle
   C) rose
   D) root
18. The tool with a flat stiff blade that is pushed with the foot into the ground is the ___
   A) shovel
   B) spade
   C) basket
   D) sickle
19. Which tool is better for lifting and moving loose sand?
   A) a hoe
   B) a watering can
   C) a rake
   D) a shovel
20. A wheelbarrow carries a big load because it has a ___
   A) wheel, a tray and two handles
   B) sharp edge under the front
   C) net and a heavy wooden lid
   D) very long rope for pulling it
21. In loading a barrow, the heavy things are put ___
   A) under the wheel
   B) near the wheel and over it
   C) at the back by the handles
   D) on top of everything
22. A wheelbarrow must never be used for carrying ___
   A) a bag of manure
   B) sand or soil
   C) people or children
   D) the harvest
23. A woven carrier used for the harvest and put on the head is a ___
   A) basket
   B) watering can
   C) shovel
   D) sprayer
24. Before we swing a cutlass we must first ___
   A) water the grass around it
   B) count the leaves on the bush
   C) hide our tools
   D) clear the people and the stones away
25. A tool is kept after work by ___
   A) burying it deep in the planting bed
   B) cleaning off the mud, drying and hanging it
   C) leaving it there on the wet ground
   D) pouring cold water on the blade
26. The tool used to fan chaff from grain is the ___
   A) cutlass
   B) watering can
   C) winnowing basket
   D) spade
27. A knapsack sprayer differs from a watering can because it sends a ___
   A) big stream of water on the root
   B) whole load of clean soil
   C) heavy heap of farm manure
   D) fine mist of medicine on to the leaf
28. A sickle is a curved one-hand blade used for ___
   A) carrying water
   B) reaping grass and maize
   C) digging a big pit
   D) tying the yam
29. The farmers' saying about the two tools that look alike is: a spade digs, a shovel ___
   A) dries
   B) sings
   C) moves
   D) sleeps
30. Tools must not be left where a child can step on them, so they are ___
   A) racked or hung with the edge covered
   B) stood up out in the hot sun
   C) thrown down inside the gutter
   D) hidden under the young crop

## Section B — Sub-objective (10 marks)

32. Mention five works of a crop farmer in order.
33. Name four things to look for on a crop farm during a visit.
34. Draw and name the parts of a tool: the working metal and the part in the hand.
35. Mention the parts of a watering can.
36. Name four tools used for carrying on the farm.
37. Sort these into digging and clearing: hoe, cutlass, spade, rake.
38. Mention the parts of a wheelbarrow.
39. Name two pairs of tools that look alike and the one difference in each.
40. Give four rules for storing farm tools well.

Answer in full sentences.

43. Name four farm tools and say what each is used for. (2 marks)
44. Explain the difference between a spade and a shovel, and between a hoe and a rake. (2 marks)
45. Write on the care and storage of farm tools. (2 marks)

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — SOCIAL AND CITIZENSHIP STUDIES (SCS) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. Marriage is the day when a man and a woman are joined as ___
   A) buyer and seller
   B) neighbours only
   C) husband and wife
   D) classmates and friends
2. Marriage is important to us because it is how a ___ begins.
   A) family
   B) school
   C) town
   D) market
3. The marriage done with signed papers before a registrar is a ___
   A) traditional marriage
   B) church marriage
   C) naming rite
   D) court marriage
4. A marriage ceremony done in a mosque or a church is called a ___
   A) court marriage
   B) naming marriage
   C) religious marriage
   D) market marriage
5. The woman on her wedding day is called the ___
   A) aunt
   B) bride
   C) daughter only
   D) groom
6. Two duties that hold a marriage together are love and ___
   A) respect
   B) sleeping
   C) fighting
   D) selling
7. Parenting means ___
   A) building new houses for the rich
   B) selling our goods in the market
   C) feeding, teaching and caring for the children
   D) driving to school every single morning
8. Which of these is NOT a duty of parents?
   A) making sure the child is clothed well
   B) doing the child's own school homework for him
   C) paying the school fees every term
   D) giving the children their daily food
9. A grown-up who keeps a child that is not his own is called a ___
   A) guardian
   B) visitor
   C) stranger
   D) classmate
10. A good parent teaches the child ___
   A) how to run away
   B) how to lie
   C) how to hide things
   D) right from wrong
11. In the family, providing for the home and protecting it is the role of the ___
   A) neighbour
   B) baby
   C) father
   D) driver
12. Keeping the home in order, cooking and caring for the little ones is the role of the ___
   A) mother
   B) watchman
   C) visitor
   D) cousin
13. A child's role in the family includes obeying, helping and ___
   A) spending the money
   B) studying well
   C) closing the gate
   D) selling the food
14. Grandparents help the family by giving advice, stories and ___
   A) punishment
   B) new clothes
   C) school fees always
   D) wisdom
15. Uncles, aunties and cousins who lend a hand are called the ___
   A) age grade
   B) nuclear class
   C) extended family
   D) market group
16. Culture is best described as ___
   A) the way of life of a people
   B) a kind of food only
   C) the name of a street
   D) a school rule
17. Which of these is NOT an element of culture?
   A) festivals and greetings
   B) the price of fuel
   C) dressing
   D) language
18. The cloth tied on a Yoruba woman's head is the ___
   A) buba
   B) fila
   C) hula
   D) gele
19. The head tie or cap worn by men in the north is called a ___
   A) fila
   B) iro
   C) gele
   D) wrapper
20. Amele, eba and pounded yam belong to Nigerian ___
   A) music
   B) food
   C) dressing
   D) names
21. The Yoruba way of greeting an elder by a boy is to ___
   A) shout
   B) run
   C) clap only
   D) prostrate
22. A kneeling greeting for an elder is done by a Yoruba ___
   A) stranger
   B) dog
   C) girl
   D) driver
23. Nigeria is found in the ___ part of Africa.
   A) western
   B) northern only
   C) eastern
   D) southern only
24. The capital city of Nigeria is ___
   A) Lagos
   B) Abuja
   C) Ibadan
   D) Kano
25. Nigeria has ___ states and one Federal Capital Territory.
   A) fifty
   B) twenty
   C) thirty-six
   D) twelve
26. The official language used in our schools in Nigeria is ___
   A) Hindi only
   B) French
   C) Arabic
   D) English
27. Our national motto says "Unity and Faith, ___ and Progress."
   A) Prayer
   B) Peace
   C) Plenty
   D) Power
28. Telling the one in charge about an open pit you saw is called ___
   A) making a noise
   B) telling a story
   C) reporting a danger
   D) hiding a matter
29. An attitude is best described as ___

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. "Papá ìdárayá" in our school means the ___
   A) fence
   B) garden
   C) kitchen
   D) playground

   A) tébèèlì
   B) ìwé
   C) ọ̀nà
   D) pátákó

   A) fence round a place

   B) màlúù
   C) ewúrẹ́
   D) agbo

   B) ọ̀wọ́ọ̀n
   C) ewúrẹ́
   D) àdìẹ

   B) eighteen
   C) eleven

   B) ọ̀kànlá

   B) j
   C) v
   D) c

   B) ü

   B) ọ̀nà

   A) shoes
   B) character
   C) face only

   B) etí
   C) ọwọ́
   D) ojú

   A) smallest child
   B) visitor at the gate
   C) driver outside
   D) eldest person present
19. When an elder is speaking to the assembly, a child should ___
   A) walk out and go and play ball
   B) stand or sit quite quiet and listen
   C) add his own noise to the talk
   D) answer the elder very loudly

   A) gígùn
   B) òkè
   C) kékeré
   D) tútù

   A) súnmọ́
   B) ẹ̀yìn
   C) ọ̀tún
   D) gbígbóná

   A) front only
   B) back only

   D) right side

   C) àkọ́
   D) oríkì

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — MATHEMATICS & ENGLISH · LITERACY (LANGUAGE DOMAIN) (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A capital letter is used at ___
   A) the end of a word
   B) before every small letter
   C) the middle of a name
   D) the start of a sentence
2. A sentence is written correctly when it begins with ___
   A) capitals on every word
   B) a small letter and ends with a comma
   C) a capital letter and ends with a full stop
   D) a full stop and no spaces
3. The word that is always a capital, wherever it stands, is ___
   A) I
   B) and
   C) a
   D) the
4. The first letter of the name of a month must be ___
   A) crossed
   B) a small letter
   C) a dot
   D) a capital
5. A double consonant in a word means ___
   A) a silent letter
   B) three letters, one sound
   C) the same consonant written twice
   D) two vowels together
6. Which word has a double consonant?
   A) spoon
   B) rabbit
   C) table
   D) chair
7. The sound in "egg" is written with ___
   A) gg
   B) cc
   C) dd
   D) ck
8. English writes "comb" with a b because ___
   A) it came from a Yoruba root word
   B) b is always written at word end
   C) the letter b is sounded twice over
   D) the b was silent, the spelling stayed
9. A silent letter is a letter that is ___
   A) spoken twice
   B) written but not spoken
   C) always a vowel
   D) used to begin a sentence
10. The long a sound in "rain" is written ___
   A) ay
   B) ea
   C) ai
   D) ee
11. The long a sound at the end of a word is usually written ___
   A) ee
   B) ai
   C) ea
   D) ay
12. Which spelling is correct?
   A) train
   B) trane
   C) traine
   D) trayn
13. The words "bee", "tree" and "see" use the sound ___
   A) long a with ai
   B) short i
   C) long e with ee
   D) silent e
14. "Sea" and "see" sound the same but are ___
   A) rhyming words
   B) spelled differently
   C) both names
   D) both numbers
15. The long i sound in "night" is written ___
   A) igh
   B) eye
   C) ie
   D) y
16. "Boy" and "toy" end with the sound written ___
   A) ou
   B) oi
   C) oy
   D) ow
17. The word "coin" is spelled with ___
   A) oy at the end
   B) ie
   C) igh
   D) oi in the middle
18. "Out", "shout" and "house" use the sound written ___
   A) oi
   B) ou
   C) ai
   D) ow
19. "Cow", "how" and "now" end with the sound written ___
   A) igh
   B) ou
   C) ow
   D) oi
20. A trigraph is ___
   A) three letters making one sound
   B) two letters making one sound
   C) three separate sounds
   D) a silent letter
21. "Care" and "chair" show that ___
   A) care has a silent letter c
   B) are and air make the same sound
   C) air has two vowel sounds
   D) are is longer than air is
22. The trigraph tch is written after ___
   A) a full stop
   B) a capital
   C) a long vowel
   D) a short vowel
23. Which word carries the trigraph ure?
   A) picture
   B) centre
   C) little
   D) proper
24. We use "an" before a word that begins with ___
   A) any letter
   B) a consonant sound
   C) a vowel sound
   D) a capital only
25. Which phrase is correct?
   A) some honest mans
   B) an honest man
   C) a honest man
   D) an house of egg
26. "Give me ____ water" — the correct word is ___
   A) one
   B) a
   C) an
   D) some
27. Which is correct: "I have ____ (4) pens"?
   A) four
   B) four's
   C) fore
   D) for
28. "There" and "their" are different because ___
   A) both of them are number words
   B) there is a place, their is belonging
   C) their is a place to sit down
   D) they sound nothing at all alike
29. The word "was" tells us something that ___
   A) is a colour
   B) is a question
   C) already happened
   D) will happen tomorrow
30. "Like" and "hide" both have ___
   A) two full stops
   B) a silent k
   C) a short i sound
   D) a long i sound

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A CVC word is made of ___

   D) five letters

   B) but
   C) boat
   D) bit

   B) -an
   C) -en
   D) -in

   B) vowel
   C) consonant cluster
   D) digraph

   B) nt
   C) st
   D) mp

   A) -ft
   B) -st
   C) -nd
   D) -nt

   C) nest
   D) lamp

   A) -mp
   B) -nd
   C) -ft
   D) -nk

   D) bank

   B) cl

   D) fl

   C) sound the same at the end

   B) ten
   C) pin
   D) pan

   A) counting its lines
   B) copying a picture
   C) sounding out every letter
   D) knowing it at once

   A) in
   B) on
   C) at
   D) and

   A) is
   B) and
   C) my
   D) the

   B) run and can

   B) small letter
   C) comma
   D) full stop

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — MATHEMATICS & ENGLISH · NUMERACY (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. Eleven is the same as ___
   A) one one and one ten only
   B) one ten and one one
   C) two tens and one one
   D) ten tens and one
2. In the number 16, the digit 1 stands for ___
   A) one ten
   B) sixteen ones
   C) one one
   D) six tens
3. The number that comes just before 20 is ___
   A) 21
   B) 18
   C) 19
   D) 11
4. Two tens and no ones written as a numeral is ___
   A) 12
   B) 22
   C) 2
   D) 20
5. Skip counting by tens gives 10, 20, 30, ___ and 50.
   A) 40
   B) 41
   C) 35
   D) 45
6. Forty-seven is made of ___
   A) forty and one
   B) four tens and seven ones
   C) seven tens and four ones
   D) four and seven
7. The correct sign for 27 ____ 34 is ___
   A) +
   B) =
   C) >
   D) <
8. Ten more than 36 is ___
   A) 46
   B) 26
   C) 37
   D) 63
9. Eighty-six is written as ___
   A) 68
   B) 60
   C) 86
   D) 18
10. The number just after 99 is ___
   A) 98
   B) 100
   C) 90
   D) 109
11. In 100 there are ___
   A) ten tens
   B) ten ones
   C) one hundred tens
   D) one hundred hundreds
12. Arrange these from the largest: 45, 39, 61, 57. ___
   A) 57, 61, 45, 39
   B) 45, 61, 39, 57
   C) 61, 57, 45, 39
   D) 39, 45, 57, 61
13. Seven tens and five ones make ___
   A) 57
   B) 12
   C) 70
   D) 75
14. The number between 69 and 71 is ___
   A) 68
   B) 70
   C) 71
   D) 72
15. The correct sign for 39 ____ 41 is ___
   A) >
   B) −
   C) <
   D) =
16. In ascending order, which comes first among 44, 27, 39 and 51?
   A) 27
   B) 51
   C) 44
   D) 39
17. 6 + 7 = ___
   A) 12
   B) 11
   C) 14
   D) 13
18. 18 − 5 = ___
   A) 23
   B) 13
   C) 15
   D) 12
19. If 9 + 4 = 13, then 13 − 9 = ___
   A) 4
   B) 9
   C) 5
   D) 13
20. In 2 + □ = 6, the number in the box is ___
   A) 3
   B) 6
   C) 8
   D) 4
21. In □ + 5 = 12, the number in the box is ___
   A) 6
   B) 5
   C) 7
   D) 17
22. In 9 − □ = 4, the number in the box is ___
   A) 9
   B) 5
   C) 13
   D) 4
23. In □ − 3 = 7, the number in the box is ___
   A) 10
   B) 7
   C) 4
   D) 21
24. Our money in Nigeria is the ___
   A) kobo coin only
   B) naira note only
   C) cedi
   D) Naira
25. ₦20 + ₦20 + ₦10 makes ___
   A) ₦30
   B) ₦40
   C) ₦50
   D) ₦60
26. A biscuit costs ₦10 and you pay with ₦50; your change is ___
   A) ₦10
   B) ₦40
   C) ₦50
   D) ₦60
27. Ten five-naira notes together make ___
   A) ₦100
   B) ₦10
   C) ₦15
   D) ₦50
28. The ₦1 and ₦2 coins are still ___
   A) our money, though seldom seen now
   B) only kept inside a bank
   C) not money at all today
   D) worth more than a ₦100 note
29. On the number line, 40 + 25 lands you at ___
   A) 70
   B) 60
   C) 65
   D) 55
30. Which is bigger, 74 or 69, and what did you look at first?
   A) 69, the tens digit
   B) 74, the tens digit
   C) 69, the ones digit
   D) 74, the ones digit

## Section B — Sub-objective (10 marks)
Answer briefly.

31. Write the numbers 11 to 20 in figures.
32. Count in tens from ten to one hundred.
33. Give the tens and the ones in 63 and in 91.
34. Arrange in descending order: 51, 24, 66, 39.
35. Put in the correct sign: 45 ____ 54, 72 ____ 27, 30 + 20 ____ 50.
36. Solve: 8 + 6, 15 − 7, 9 + 9 and 20 − 6.
37. There are 11 oranges and 6 bananas. How many fruits, and what operation did you use?
38. Fill the boxes: 7 + □ = 13, □ − 8 = 6 and 14 − □ = 6.
39. Count the money: ₦20 + ₦10 + ₦5 + ₦5, and ₦50 − ₦20.
40. Name four notes of Nigeria from the smallest and two coins we count with.

## Section C — Theory (10 marks)
Answer in full sentences.

41. Write the numbers from 31 to 50 and say how many tens and ones are in 47. (2 marks)
42. Explain the difference between > and <, and arrange 38, 83, 33 and 88 from the smallest. (2 marks)
43. Solve these and show your work: 12 + 5, 17 − 6, 9 + 8, 20 − 13. (2 marks)
44. What is an open sentence? Find the box in 6 + □ = 15 and in □ − 7 = 8. (2 marks)
45. Write on the naira: the coins and notes you know, and how you pay ₦50 for a thing that costs ₦35. (2 marks)

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — GENERAL KNOWLEDGE (Nursery 2 · 2nd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 50 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. The helper who treats people when they are sick is the ___
   A) tailor
   B) farmer
   C) doctor
   D) driver
2. A nurse works mainly at the ___
   A) market
   B) hospital or clinic
   C) filling station
   D) farm
3. The helper who sews our school cloth is the ___
   A) tailor
   B) barber
   C) mason
   D) pilot
4. Where does a police officer work? He works at the ___
   A) school kitchen
   B) motor park
   C) police station
   D) poultry house
5. The farmer is important to the town because he ___
   A) drives the bus
   B) grows the food we eat
   C) keeps the gate
   D) sews our uniforms
6. Our money in Nigeria is called the ___
   A) franc
   B) shilling
   C) cedi
   D) Naira
7. The sign written for the Naira is ___
   A) ₦
   B) $
   C) £
   D) ¢
8. The colours of the Nigerian flag are ___
   A) white and blue only
   B) green and gold
   C) green, white and green
   D) red, white and black
9. The white colour on our flag stands for ___
   A) water
   B) market
   C) farms
   D) peace
10. The green colour on our flag is for our ___
   A) planes and birds
   B) farms and land
   C) books and pens
   D) cars and roads
11. Our capital city is ___
   A) Abuja
   B) Sokoto
   C) Ibadan
   D) Lagos
12. On a simple map, our country Nigeria is found in ___ Africa.
   A) East
   B) South only
   C) North only
   D) West
13. The eagle is important to us because it is our ___
   A) pet in the house
   B) national bird
   C) fish of the river
   D) farm animal
14. The animal called the king of animals is the ___
   A) rabbit
   B) goat
   C) lion
   D) dog
15. The home of a dog is called a ___
   A) kennel
   B) coop
   C) hutch
   D) stable
16. A chicken is kept in a ___
   A) nest on a tall tree
   B) coop or a poultry house
   C) sty or a pig hole
   D) stable for a horse
17. The language used for our lessons in school is ___
   A) Latin
   B) French
   C) Arabic only
   D) English
18. A day with clouds and water falling from the sky is a ___
   A) windy day
   B) sunny day
   C) rainy day
   D) dusty day
19. The harmattan season in Nigeria brings air that is ___
   A) wet and muddy
   B) dry, dusty and cool
   C) dark all day
   D) hot and rainy
20. On a rainy morning the thing we carry to keep dry is an ___
   A) plate
   B) fan
   C) broom
   D) umbrella
21. The part of a plant that drinks water from the soil is the ___
   A) root
   B) flower
   C) fruit
   D) leaf
22. A flower on a plant later becomes the ___
   A) branch
   B) stone
   C) fruit
   D) shadow
23. The things a plant gives us for our home are ___
   A) iron and tin
   B) books and pens
   C) only fire and ash
   D) food, shade and fresh air
24. Which of these is a vegetable and not a fruit?
   A) ugu
   B) mango
   C) banana
   D) orange
25. We hold a grown-up's ___ when we cross a busy road.
   A) bag
   B) hand
   C) shoe
   D) finger only
26. Before crossing the road a child should first ___
   A) shout at all of the cars
   B) close both eyes and then jump
   C) stop and look left, right, then left
   D) run across as fast as possible
27. The sense we use for smelling food is the ___
   A) nose
   B) ear
   C) tongue
   D) skin
28. A dangerous thing at home that must never be touched is ___
   A) a folded towel on the bed
   B) a school shoe by the door
   C) a wooden spoon for the pot
   D) a bottle of medicine on the shelf
29. The tortoise in our folktales is known for being clever and ___
   A) big as an elephant
   B) greedy
   C) fast always
   D) strong like a horse
30. In the story of the wisdom gourd, the tortoise learnt that wisdom must be ___
   A) sold at the market
   B) hidden in a tree
   C) shared with everybody
   D) buried in the farm

## Section B — Sub-objective (10 marks)
Answer briefly.

31. Name five helpers in our community and what each does.
32. Say where four of these helpers work: doctor, teacher, police, farmer.
33. Name the two colours of our flag and what each means.
34. Mention four Nigerian foods and two clothes we wear on special days.
35. Name the two seasons in Nigeria and one thing each brings.
36. Name four parts of a plant and what the root does.
37. Match four animals with their homes: dog, fowl, cow, rabbit.
38. Name the five senses with the organ of each.
39. Mention four dangers of the home we must keep away from.
40. Name three songs, rhymes or tales we learnt this term.

## Section C — Theory (10 marks)
Answer in full sentences.

41. Who are community helpers? Write on four of them and why we thank them. (2 marks)
42. Describe the Nigerian flag, our money and where our country is. (2 marks)
43. Write on the weather: the two seasons of Nigeria and the clothes for each. (2 marks)
44. Name plants around you: four parts of a plant and what each part does. (2 marks)
45. Tell a story or a rhyme you learnt this term and say what it teaches. (2 marks)
